(1.3.14)

where the
p
maps are dequantization maps and
v
is a map of BD algebras. Furthermore there are maps
\pi
from the respective graph to ribbon graph complexes, which respect the algebraic structures and are compatible as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}\llbracket\hbar\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathcal{G}\llbracket\hbar\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{RT}_{\Lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{RG}_{\Lambda}\llbracket\gamma\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathcal{RG}_{\Lambda}\llbracket\gamma\rrbracket.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}

(1.3.15)

There are explicit non-zero
Maurer-Cartan elements

D\in MCE(\mathcal{RT}_{\Lambda})\ \text{and}\ T\in MCE(\mathcal{T})

in the respective shifted Poisson algebras; further there are explicit non-zero

S\in MCE(s\mathcal{RG}_{\Lambda}\llbracket\gamma\rrbracket)\ \text{and}\ G\in MCE(s\mathcal{G}\llbracket\hbar\rrbracket)

in the respective BD algebras. Those are all compatible under the maps from diagram (
1.3.15
).

To explain the relevance of these specific Maurer-Cartan elements and their relationship with
quantization
we need to elaborate:
given a collection of cyclic chain complexes
V_{\Lambda}
we denote

\mathcal{F}^{fr}(V_{\Lambda}):=\Big(Sym(Cyc^{*}_{+}(V_{\Lambda})[-1]),\cdot,d,\{\_,\_\}_{o}\Big),

the ‘
free open observables
’,
which is a shifted Poisson algebra; see around equation (
6.1.6
) and note the differences to the object from equation (
1.3.1
). Further there is
a dequantization map

p:\mathcal{F}^{pq}(V_{\Lambda})\rightarrow\mathcal{F}^{fr}(V_{\Lambda})

from the ‘
prequantum open observables
’ to the ‘
free open observables
’. A well-known result (see lemma
6.1.4
) is that an odd dimensional cyclic
A_{\infty}
-category
\Lambda
with underlying collection of cyclic chain complexes
V_{\Lambda}
induces a Maurer-Cartan element

I\in MCE(\mathcal{F}^{fr}(V_{\Lambda})),

(1.3.16)

also called hamiltonian of the cyclic
A_{\infty}
-category.

Definition 1.3.17
.

A
quantization
of a cyclic
A_{\infty}
-category
\Lambda
is a Maurer-Cartan element

I^{q}\in\mathcal{F}^{pq}(V_{\Lambda})\ \ \text{such that}\ \ p(I^{q})=I.

(1.3.18)

Remark 1.3.19
.

Quantizations of cyclic
A_{\infty}
-categories are sometimes called quantum
A_{\infty}
-categories.
Equivalently they may be described as a possibly curved (involutive bi-Lie
)_{\infty}
-algebra structure on a central extension of cyclic cochains of this category
[
20
,
25
]
.

We can provide an interpretation for the Maurer-Cartan elements from theorem F as being the geometric analogues of the notion of hamiltonian of a cyclic
A_{\infty}
-category and of their quantizations:

Theorem G.1

(see theorem
10.1.19
and lemmas
10.1.20
and
10.1.21
)
.

Let
\Lambda
be an odd dimensional cyclic
A_{\infty}
-category with underlying collection of cyclic chain complexes
V_{\Lambda}
.
Denote by
{I\in MCE(\mathcal{F}^{fr}(V_{\Lambda}))}
its associated hamiltonian.
This datum induces the two left vertical maps of shifted Poisson algebras respectively of Beilinson-Drinfeld algebras in the diagram below.
Given a quantization of
\Lambda
, denoted
I^{q}\in MCE(F^{pq}(V_{\Lambda}))
, we further get the right vertical map of BD algebras, compatible as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}^{fr}(V_{\Lambda})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}^{pq}(V_{\Lambda})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}^{pq}(V_{\Lambda})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{RT}_{\Lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{RG}_{\Lambda}\llbracket\gamma\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathcal{RG}_{\Lambda}\llbracket\gamma\rrbracket.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{I^{q}}}

(1.3.20)

Furthermore for the
Maurer-Cartan elements
from theorem F we have that

\rho_{I}(D)=I\ \text{and }\ \rho_{I^{q}}(S)=I^{q}.

There is a totally parallel story for cyclic
L_{\infty}
-algebras: indeed for a cyclic
L_{\infty}
-algebra
L
we can introduce the notion of hamiltonian, which is given by a Maurer-Cartan element
I
in a shifted Poisson algebra
\mathcal{O}^{fr}(V)
, built from the underlying cyclic chain complex
V
of
L
; see around equation (
7.1.1
). There is a dequantization map, see definition
7.2.3
,

p:\mathcal{O}^{pq}(V)\rightarrow\mathcal{O}^{fr}(V).

A
quantization
of an odd dimensional cyclic
L_{\infty}
-algebra
L
is a Maurer-Cartan element

I^{q}\in\mathcal{O}^{pq}(V)\ \ \text{such that}\ \ p(I^{q})=I.

(1.3.21)

These notions provide an interpretation of the other Maurer-Cartan elements in theorem F as being the geometric analogues of the notion of hamiltonian of a cyclic
L_{\infty}
-algebra and of their quantizations:

Theorem G.2

(see theorem
10.2.20
and lemmas
10.2.21
,
10.2.22
)
.

Let
L
be a cyclic
L_{\infty}
-algebra with underlying cyclic chain complex
V
.
Denote by
I\in MCE(\mathcal{O}^{fr}(V))
its associated hamiltonian.
This datum induces the two left vertical maps of shifted Poisson algebras respectively of Beilinson-Drinfeld algebras in the diagram below.
Given a quantization of
L
, denoted
I^{q}\in MCE(\mathcal{O}^{pq}(V))
, we further get the right vertical map of BD algebras, compatible as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}^{fr}(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}^{pq}(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}^{pq}(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}^{d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}^{d}\llbracket\hbar\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathcal{G}^{d}\llbracket\hbar\rrbracket.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{I^{q}}}

Furthermore for the Maurer-Cartan elements from theorem F we have that

\theta_{I}(T)=I\ \text{and }\ \theta_{I^{q}}(G)=I^{q}.

Our next theorem provides a very satisfying characterization of the twisted (ribbon) tree complexes as universal sources of domain and target of the classical LQT map, that is cyclic cochains of a cyclic
A_{\infty}
-category respectively Lie algebra cochains of a cyclic
L_{\infty}
-algebras:

Theorem I

(see theorems
11.0.1
,
10.2.23
)
.

Given an essentially finite odd dimensional cyclic
A_{\infty}
-category
\Lambda
there is a commutative diagram of shifted Poisson algebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Big(Sym\big(Cyc^{*}_{+}(\Lambda)[-1]\big),d_{cyc},\{\_,\_\}\Big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Big(C_{+}^{*}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N}A_{\Lambda}),d_{CE},\{\_,\_\}\Big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{RT}^{tw}_{\Lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}^{tw},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces LQT}

functorial with respect to strict cyclic
A_{\infty}
-morphisms. The (ribbon) tree complexes are now
twisted
by the Maurer-Cartan elements from theorem F. The vertical right map is defined also for cyclic
L_{\infty}
-algebra not induced by a cyclic
A_{\infty}
-categories.

It would be interesting to explore whether these universal sources induce some canonical cohomology classes and to find a different interpretation of those.

Remark 1.3.22
.

A natural question is how far the construction from theorem I can be made homotopically coherent.
One may try to generalize theorem I to proper Calabi-Yau categories, but allowing shifted Poisson
\infty
-algebras and maps of those.
24
24

24

It is to be expected that proper Calabi-Yau categories can be strictified to a cyclic one. See theorem 10.2.2 of
[
66
]
and
[
23
]
for a proof in the algebra case and theorem 2.34 of
[
3
]
for the category case, but where additionally smoothness, see definition
4.3.22
, is required.

It is an immediate question to find a quantized version of theorem I, which we tackle now: we denote the left upper corner of the diagram in theorem I by
\mathcal{F}^{cl}(\Lambda)
and the right upper corner by
\mathcal{O}^{cl}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N}A_{\Lambda}),
the ‘
classical observables
’. Given a quantization of a cyclic
A_{\infty}
-category
{I^{q}\in\mathcal{F}^{pq}(V_{\Lambda})}
we denote the twist of
\mathcal{F}^{pq}(V_{\Lambda})
by that Maurer-Cartan element by
\mathcal{F}^{q}(\Lambda)
and we denote the twist of
\mathcal{O}^{pq}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N}A_{\Lambda})
by the determined quantization of the cyclic
L_{\infty}
-algebra by
\mathcal{O}^{q}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N}A_{\Lambda})
, the ‘
quantum observables
’.

With this we present a quantized version of theorem I, which is a main result in this thesis:

Theorem J

(see theorem
8.2.17
and theorem
11.0.1
)
.

Given a quantization of an essentially finite odd dimensional cyclic
A_{\infty}
-category, there is a commutative diagram of
BD
algebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}^{q}(\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}^{q}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N}A_{\Lambda})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathcal{RG}^{tw}_{\Lambda}\llbracket\gamma\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathcal{G}^{tw}\llbracket\hbar\rrbracket,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces LQT^{q}_{N}}

functorial with respect to strict cyclic
A_{\infty}
-morphisms of quantizations of cyclic
A_{\infty}
-categories. Furthermore for
N\rightarrow\infty
the map
LQT^{q}_{N}
becomes a quasi-isomorphism. Lastly there are dequantization maps to theorem I as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}^{cl}(\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}^{cl}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N}A_{\Lambda})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}^{q}(\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}^{q}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N}A_{\Lambda})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{RT}^{tw}_{\Lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}^{tw}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathcal{RG}^{tw}_{\Lambda}\llbracket\gamma\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathcal{G}^{tw}\llbracket\hbar\rrbracket,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces LQT^{cl}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces LQT^{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{I}^{tw}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{I_{N}}^{tw}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{I^{q}}^{tw}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{I^{q}_{N}}^{tw}}

that is the 4 maps between the front and back face are dequantization maps.

1.3.2
Open-Closed Circle-Action Formality Morphism

In the previous section
1.3.1
we analyzed in great details the ‘open analogue’ of the representing map (
1.2.8
) connecting the closed universal geometric BD algebra (
1.2.6
) with the closed algebraic BD algebra (
5.1.1
). This closed representing map was crucial to define the categorical Gromov-Witten potential (
1.2.13
) in section
1.2
, for which we further used the canonical Maurer-Cartan element in the closed geometric BD algebra (
1.2.6
), the string vertices (
1.2.9
).

We explained in the previous section that there is
no analogous Maurer-Cartan element
in the open universal geometric BD algebra from theorem B, which we quantified in theorem G.1.

As a step towards defining an
open categorical Gromov-Witten potential
, we ask if it is anyways possible to obtain a
quantization
of a cyclic
A_{\infty}
-category. Based on the preprint
[
93
]
, we propose to consider the combined open-closed theory to provide an answer.

Given a cyclic
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{C}
and a full subcategory
\Lambda\subseteq\mathcal{C}
the ‘
open-closed observables
’ are given by the tensor product of the closed BD algebra (
1.2.1
) and the open BD algebra (
1.3.1
),
25
25

25

which exists, see lemma
4.1.19
.

\mathcal{F}^{c}(\mathcal{C})\otimes\mathcal{F}^{o}(\Lambda).

(1.3.23)

Similarly we can consider

\mathcal{F}^{c}(\mathcal{C})^{Triv}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{o}(\Lambda),

recalling the abelian BD algebra from (
1.2.4
).
The next main result in this thesis is following open-closed version of the closed
trivializing morphism
from equation (
1.2.11
):

Theorem K

(corollary
12.3.29
)
.

Given a cyclic
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{C}
, a set of objects
\Lambda\subset\mathcal{C}
and a splitting
s
of the non-commutative Hodge filtration. Then there is an
L_{\infty}
quasi-isomorphism

\Psi_{s}^{oc}:\ \mathcal{F}^{c}(\mathcal{C})\otimes\mathcal{F}^{o}(\Lambda)\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{F}^{c}(\mathcal{C})^{Triv}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{o}(\Lambda).

(1.3.24)

Remark 1.3.25
.

The proof of this theorem reduces to proving an identity (theorem
12.3.16
) for the Taylor components of the purely closed
L_{\infty}
-morphism (
1.2.11
). It would be interesting to have an operadic interpretation of this identity, perhaps in terms of
BV_{\infty}
-algebras
[
49
]
.

Remark 1.3.26
.

As is typical in this area of mathematical physics the morphism (
1.3.24
) is defined in terms of graphs. To verify theorem K we prove an interesting identity (equation
12.3.15
) about the ‘moduli space of graphs’, which is about relating graphs with varying number of vertices to each other, with the additional choice of a partition of the half-edges of a given vertex.

We now explain how theorem K provides a building stone in understanding when and how to obtain a quantization of a given
smooth
cyclic
A_{\infty}
-category, or for simplicity a given smooth cyclic
A_{\infty}
-algebra
End_{\mathcal{C}}(\lambda)
.

For that we introduce the map

\nu\langle ch(\lambda),\_\rangle_{res}:\mathcal{F}^{c}(\mathcal{C})\otimes\mathcal{F}^{o}(\lambda)\rightarrow\mathcal{F}^{c}(\mathcal{C})\otimes\mathcal{F}^{o}(\lambda),

the derivation induced by pairing the non-commutative Chern character
26
26

26

defined as the class of the unit of
End(\lambda)
in negative cyclic chains

ch(\lambda)
via the residue pairing (
1.2.2
), further weighted by
\nu
(the basis of
Cyc^{0}(\lambda)[-1]
cyclic cochains of length zero).

Our following results are valid for tuples
(\mathcal{C},\lambda)
of smooth cyclic categories
\mathcal{C}
and
\lambda\in\mathcal{C}
satisfying a assumption
(*)
. Forthcoming work
[
4
]
combined with the fundamental work
[
99
,
62
]
about the open-closed string vertices will show that this assumption is satisfied for all such tuples; see section
13.1
for some more context.

Assumption
(*)
.

Given a smooth cyclic
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{C}
and an object
{\lambda\in\mathcal{C}}
there is an element
S^{oc}\in\mathcal{F}^{c}(\mathcal{C})\otimes\mathcal{F}^{o}(\lambda)
that is a Maurer-Cartan element up an extra term coming form the Chern character of
\lambda

(d_{hoch}+uB+\gamma\Delta_{c}+d+\gamma\delta+\nabla)S^{oc}+\frac{1}{2}\{S^{oc},S^{oc}\}_{o}+\frac{1}{2}\{S^{oc},S^{oc}\}_{c}+\nu\langle ch(\lambda),S^{oc}\rangle_{res}=0,

(1.3.27)

and the open tree level part
27
27

27

That is the projection to
Sym^{0}(\cdots)\otimes Sym^{1}(Cyc^{*}(\lambda)[-1])\gamma^{0}
.
of
S^{oc}
is the hamiltonian
I
of the cyclic
A_{\infty}
-algebra
End_{\mathcal{C}}(\lambda),

I:=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\langle m^{k}(\_,\cdots,\_),\_\rangle\in Cyc^{*}(\lambda)[-1].

(1.3.28)

If we assume that the Chern character is homotopically trivial we can ‘gauge away’ the last summand of equation (
1.3.27
), which is our proposition
13.2.1
:
given a smooth cyclic
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{C}
and
{\lambda\in\mathcal{C}}
that satisfy assumption
(*)
and such that
ch(\lambda)=(d_{hoch}+uB)h
. Denote

S^{oc,q}(\lambda,h):=e^{\nu\langle h,-\rangle_{M}}S^{oc}

Then we have that

S^{oc,q}(\lambda,h)\in QME\big(\mathcal{F}^{c}(\mathcal{C})\otimes\mathcal{F}^{o}(\lambda)\big).

(1.3.29)

Further if we change
h
by an exact term
S^{oc,q}(\lambda,h)
changes up to homotopy.

Definition 1.3.30
.

We call a
package
the datum of a smooth cyclic
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{C}
together with the choice of an object
\lambda\in\mathcal{C}
of that category, the choice of a trivialization
(d_{hoch}+uB)h=ch(\lambda)
and a splitting
s

\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{C},\lambda,h,s}:=\{\mathcal{C},\lambda,h,s\}.

(1.3.31)

A package allows us to find the quantization we searched for: consider the map of dg Lie algebras

\pi:\mathcal{F}^{c}(\mathcal{C})^{Triv}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{o}(\Lambda)\rightarrow Sym^{0}(\cdot)\gamma^{0}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{o}(\lambda)\cong\mathcal{F}^{o}(\lambda)

and denote the image of the gauged element (
1.3.29
) under the open-closed trivializing morphism from theorem K and the map
\pi
by

S^{o,q}(\mathcal{C},\lambda,h,s):=(\pi\circ\Psi_{s}^{oc})S^{oc,q}(\lambda,h).

Then we can find an
‘open’ quantization
:

Theorem L

(theorem
13.2.7
)
.

Given a package
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{C},\lambda,h,s}
from definition
1.3.30
such that assumption
(*)
holds then
S^{o,q}(\mathcal{C},\lambda,h,s)
is a quantization of the cyclic
A_{\infty}
-algebra
End_{\mathcal{C}}(\lambda)
.

In section
1.2
we described how one can extract from a solution to the closed Maurer-Cartan equation the closed categorical Gromov-Witten potential, which in turn allowed to extract actual numbers.
Theorem L tells us under which conditions we can find a solution to the open Quantum Master Equation.
However, we have not understood how to extract numbers from such an element at the moment. Geometrically, in open Gromov-Witten theory one imposes some extra conditions on the Lagrangians in order to extract actual numbers: eg. in
[
46
]
it is required that the Lagrangian has the homology of a sphere and in
[
83
]
that the Lagrangian is the locus of an involution. One could ponder whether similar constraints for the categorical set-up allow to extract actual numbers.

From a physical point of view
, however, theorem
L
tells us when we can find a quantization of the classical large
N
open string field theory on a stack of
N
branes of type
\lambda
, implied by the quantized LQT map; see remark
1.3.6
. Elaborating a bit, the field content of the open string field theory on a stack of
N
branes of type
\lambda
is described by the cyclic
L_{\infty}
-algebra

End_{\mathcal{C}}(\lambda^{\oplus N}).

(1.3.32)

Note that the object (
1.3.32
) only exists in categories
\mathcal{C}
that are additive. In this case we can further identify the cyclic
L_{\infty}
-algebra (
1.3.32
) (or better its Lie-algebra cochains) with the target of the LQT-map (
1.3.5
):

End_{\mathcal{C}}(\lambda^{\oplus N})\cong\mathfrak{gl}_{N}End_{\mathcal{C}}(\lambda).

(1.3.33)

Not all the categories we are interested in are additive, eg. Fukaya categories. In this case the isomorphism (
1.3.33
) allows us to view the algebra of
N\times N
matrices with values in
End_{\mathcal{C}}(\lambda)
, which also exists in non-additive categories, as a convenient replacement for
End_{\mathcal{C}}(\lambda^{\oplus N})
.

The perspective advocated in
[
33
,
7
,
54
]
is that a lift of the classical interaction term (
1.3.28
), which is the large
N
limit of the classical interaction terms associated to the cyclic
L_{\infty}
-algebras (
1.3.32
), to a solution to the open Quantum Master Equation describes, within the Batalin-Vilkovisky formalism from theoretical physics and under the quantized LQT map, the quantum partition function of a large
N
matrix model (the finite dimensional cousins of large
N
gauge theories).
28
28

28

See the exciting recent work
[
60
,
61
]
which allows to include these infinite-dimensional cases.
This was worked out in
[
54
]
for the Gaussian matrix model.

Remark 1.3.34
.

In
[
33
]
, page 3 it is argued that open string field theory in general and (large
N
) holomorphic Chern-Simons theory on
\mathbb{C}^{3}
in particular does not admit a quantization by itself, in apparent
29
29

29

Note that we can’t apply theorem L as Dolbeault forms on
\mathbb{C}^{3}
do not verify the properness assumption. See however the recent work
[
60
,
61
]
and also
[
33
]
, which may allow to loosen the properness restriction at the cost of introducing more analysis.
contrast to what we claim here. However note that the quantizations we produce in theorem L have ‘contributions’ from the closed sector by stable graphs with zero leaves and thus do depend on both the open and closed sectors, which is actually similar in spirit to the main idea of
[
33
]
. We would like to examine this further in the future.

In the context of theorem L and motivated by the physics discussion we call
partition function of the large
N
open string gauge theory on a stack of
N

\lambda
-branes

the exponential of the Maurer-Cartan element
{S^{o,q}(\mathcal{C},\lambda,h,s)\in QME(\mathcal{F}^{o}(\lambda))},

Z^{o}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{C},\lambda,h,s}):=e^{S^{o,q}(\mathcal{C},\lambda,h,s)/\gamma},

(1.3.35)

which is a closed element with respect to the differential of the BD algebra.
30
30

30

This is a general fact about BD algebras; we further should suitably localize to allow power series in
\gamma
.

1.3.3
Towards Twisted Holography from Calabi-Yau Categories

In the last part of the thesis we build upon the physics perspective laid out around equation
1.3.32
to conjecture a
relationship between the partition function of the large
N
open string gauge theory on a stack of
N

\lambda
-branes and a deformation of the closed categorical Gromov-Witten potential
, which we will introduce in the following. It is useful to recall the interpretation of the GW potential as the partition function of closed string field theory; see remark
1.2.14
. Note that despite the physics name these partition functions should be encoding enumerative invariants and indeed
our conjecture aims to give a new viewpoint on known and proposed deep relationships

[
67
,
55
,
29
]
, which we explain at the very end of this section.

The ideas presented in this section are still in an early stage and have not appeared in print; for instance some shifting conventions are yet to be fixed.

We recall that our set-up consists of a smooth cyclic
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{C}
together with the choice of ‘a brane‘, an object
\lambda\in\mathcal{C}
of that category.
31
31

31

We will work with pairs
(\mathcal{C},\lambda)
satisfying assumption
(*)
from equation (
1.3.27
). As it will be shown that
all
smooth cyclic
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{C}
with a choice of
\lambda\in\mathcal{C}
satisfy
(*)
in the forthcoming work
[
4
]
we do not mention this assumption each time in this section.
We referred to such datum with additional choice of a trivialization
(d_{hoch}+uB)h=ch(\lambda)
and a splitting
s
of the non-commutative Hodge filtration (definition
4.5.1
) as a package

\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{C},\lambda,h,s}:=\{\mathcal{C},\lambda,h,s\}.

(1.3.36)

We call
holographic
a pair consisting of a smooth cyclic
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{C}
together with an object
\lambda\in\mathcal{C}
, provided a (for the moment relatively idealistic) condition, see definition
13.3.18
.
In this case we will then also call
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{C},\lambda,h,s}
a
holography package
.

Remark 1.3.37
.

It seems suggestive that this condition or a possible weakening of it may be satisfied if the endomorphisms of the object have the rational homology of a sphere, but investigating this has to be left to future work.

Given a holography package an immediate consequence
32
32

32

We imagine that one could relax the condition considerably on what it means to be holographic in our sense, which would on the other hand make this consequence less immediate.
is that there exists a closed element

Z^{c,br}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{C},\lambda,h,s})\in\mathcal{F}^{c}(\mathcal{C})^{Triv}\llbracket\nu\rrbracket,

(1.3.38)

which we call
partition function of the closed string field theory associated to
\mathcal{C}
backreacted in the direction of
\lambda
, see definition
13.3.29
. Here
\mathcal{F}^{c}(\mathcal{C})^{Triv}\llbracket\nu\rrbracket
is the abelian BD algebra from definition
1.2.4
, extended
\nu
-linearly.

Remark 1.3.39
.

The variable
\nu
is a placeholder for the number of branes
N
. Also in physics the backreacted closed string field theory depends highly non-trivial on
N

[
79
,
55
,
29
]
.
33
33

33

In more detail, we recall that
\nu
is the basis of cyclic cochains of length zero from the open sector, keeping track of the number of free boundaries under the map from theorem B, see also around theorem
13.1.5
. Under the LQT map at rank
N

\nu
gets mapped to
N
.

Very roughly speaking the partition function
Z^{c,br}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{C},\lambda,h,s})
is determined by some deformation of the closed categorical Gromov-Witten potential from (
1.2.13
) associated to the category
\mathcal{C}
in the direction of
\lambda
.
More precisely the element
Z^{c,br}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{C},\lambda,h,s})
is the large
N
quantum partition function of a classical field theory; its defining
L_{\infty}
-algebra is obtained by twisting a
curved

L_{\infty}
-algebra on cyclic cochains of
\mathcal{C}
, whose curvature is determined by the brane
\lambda
; see equation (
1.3.41
) below.

This seems to exactly mimic the heuristic how physicists understand the
backreaction
phenomena of closed string field theory, compare the beginning of section
13.3
.
In the discussion in remark
1.3.40
below we make this more precise and compare this description closely with the treatment of backreaction of the closed B-model in Costello-Gaiotto’s approach to ‘Twisted Holography’
[
29
]
.

We come to the mentioned conjecture: in theoretical physics there is the enormously influential idea of holography, going back to the seminal work
[
79
]
. Roughly speaking holography proposes to exactly match a gravitational theory living on some bulk spacetime with a gauge theory on the boundary.
Often these two sides can be realized as a closed backreacted string field theory in the direction of some brane, respectively as an large
N
open string field theory on a stack of this brane.
Thus holography proposes that in particular the partition functions of these two theories should be identified.

Having in mind the interpretation of
Z^{c,br}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{C},\lambda,h,s})
from equation
1.3.38
as the partition function of the
closed string field theory backreacted in direction
\lambda
and the interpretation of
Z^{o}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{C},\lambda,h,s})
from equation
1.3.35
as the
large
N
open string field theory on a stack of
N
branes of type
\lambda
it seems natural to conjecture a version of
holography
in our set-up (which is about topological string theories, more precisely those induced by Calabi-Yau categories):

Conjecture H
.

Given a holography package (definition
1.3.36
) the partition function of the backreacted closed string field theory is equal to the partition function of the large
N
open string gauge theory on a stack of
N

\lambda
-branes; or more precisely the element (
1.3.38
) is homologous to the element (
1.3.35
), that is

\iota_{c}(Z^{c,br}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{C},\lambda,h,s}))\simeq\iota_{o}(Z^{o}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{C},\lambda,h,s})),

under the natural inclusion maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}^{c}(\mathcal{C})^{Triv}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{o}(\lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}^{c}(\mathcal{C})^{Triv}\llbracket\nu\rrbracket}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}^{o}(\lambda).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{o}}

We view this conjecture as a main accomplishments of this thesis. Its formulation is a step in understanding a plethora of
extremely rich and important relations in enumerative geometry

[
67
,
55
,
29
]
(and proposals thereof from physics) from a unified mathematical perspective. See also remark
13.4.2
for ideas on a refined version of this conjecture, allowing both sides to depend on natural parameters; a key step is a formality statement, see theorem
13.4.5
. We elaborate on the relation of mentioned works to conjecture H at the end of this introduction under ‘applications’.

Before that, to support our interpretation of
Z^{c,br}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{C},\lambda,h,s})
as the partition function of closed backreacted SFT, we comment on the treatment of backreaction in Costello-Gaiotto’s approach to ‘Twisted Holography’, notably their treatment of BCOV theory in section 4 of
[
29
]
. A reader not so familiar with these ideas may want to skip the long remark
1.3.40
and go directly to the proposed applications of conjecture H.

Remark 1.3.40
.

To further support our interpretation of
Z^{c,br}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{C},\lambda,h,s})
we first observe the following: the genus zero part of the element
Z^{c,br}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{C},\lambda,h,s})
describes (or rather its preimage under the
L_{\infty}
quasi-isomorphism (
1.3.24
)) for every
N\in\mathbb{N}
an
L_{\infty}
-structure on the chain complex
CC^{*}(\mathcal{C})[1]
, cyclic cochains of
\mathcal{C}
, that is on the fields of the closed string field theory, by setting
\nu=N
. We denote this
L_{\infty}
-algebra by
L_{N}^{br}(\mathcal{C},\lambda,h)
; see definition
13.3.14
for details. The
L_{\infty}
-structure
L_{N}^{br}(\mathcal{C},\lambda,h)
is roughly speaking obtained by twisting an explicit
curved

L_{\infty}
-structure
L_{N}(\mathcal{C},\lambda)
on cyclic cochains by a primitive
h
of the Chern character; see remark
13.3.16
and its preceding discussion. We explain in lemma
13.3.7
that the curvature of the mentioned curved
L_{\infty}
-algebra
L_{N}(\mathcal{C},\lambda)
is given (to leading order in
N
) by

N\langle ch(\lambda),\_\rangle_{res}\in CC^{*}(\mathcal{C})[1].

(1.3.41)

We come to Costello-Gaiotto’s treatment of backreaction in the closed B-model:

BCOV Theory
.

Costello-Gaiotto explain how the dg-Lie algebra of classical interacting BCOV theory on
\mathbb{C}^{3}
(the classical closed SFT of the B-model, see sections
5.3.2
,
5.2
, conjecture
5.3.19
)

\big(PV_{c}^{\bullet,\bullet}(\mathbb{C}^{3})\llbracket u\rrbracket,\bar{\partial}+u\partial,[\_,\_]\big)

(1.3.42)

gets modified to the curved dg-Lie algebra

\big(PV_{c}^{\bullet,\bullet}(\mathbb{C}^{3})\llbracket u\rrbracket,N\delta_{\mathbb{C}},\bar{\partial}+u\partial,\{\_,\_\}\big)

(1.3.43)

when introducing the brane
\lambda\in\mathcal{D}^{b}_{dg}(\mathbb{C}^{3})
determined by

\mathbb{C}\subset\mathbb{C}^{3}
. Twisting by a (in this situation essentially unique) Maurer-Cartan element, a Beltrami differential, they recover an uncurved dg-Lie algebra. They claim that this (uncurved) dg-Lie algebra describes (deformations of the complex structure of) the
deformed conifold
(see section 4.1 of
[
29
]
), which is the backreacted geometry of the closed string field theory on
\mathbb{C}^{3}
in the direction of the brane
\lambda
.

Inspired by Costello-Gaiotto’s work we view (respectively call) the non-curved
L_{\infty}
-structure
L_{N}^{br}(\mathcal{C},\lambda,h)
as the
classical backreacted closed string field theory in the direction of
\lambda
. See also conjecture
13.3.32
on the
existence of backreacted Calabi-Yau categories
and its surrounding discussion for more context.

Basically we would like to realize the dg Lie algebra of Costello-Gaiotto describing the deformed conifold as
L_{N}^{br}(\mathcal{C},\lambda,h)
for
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{D}^{b}_{dg}(\mathbb{C}^{3})
,
\lambda
given by
\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{C})^{\oplus N}
for
\mathbb{C}\subset\mathbb{C}^{3}
and
h
given by their Mauer-Cartan element. We run into the problem that
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{dg}(\mathbb{C}^{3})
is not proper nor cyclic, thus we cannot apply our theory.
34
34

34

Perhaps working with compactly supported polyvector fields could resolve this. In another direction, to define the structure
L_{N}^{br}(\mathcal{C},\lambda)
we are only using the genus zero part of the open-closed TCFT associated to
\mathcal{C}
; compare section
3.1
. Results in the literature
[
14
]
seem to suggest that already the structure of a smooth Calabi-Yau induces such a structure, which
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{dg}(\mathbb{C}^{3})
would satisfy.
One can identify the underlying chain complex of (
1.3.43
) and thus the underlying chain complex of the dg Lie algebra describing the deformed conifold with
CC^{*}\big(\mathcal{D}^{b}_{dg}(\mathbb{C}^{3})\big)
using an HKR isomorphism (see page 36 of
[
33
]
). Further, under this isomorphism the curvature term of (
1.3.43
) should be identified with the term (
1.3.41
).
In any case, even if we could apply our theory, see footnote in this remark, an identification (in the sense of some
L_{\infty}
quasi-isomorphism) of the whole dg Lie structures could be difficult.

The partition function of the closed backreacted string field theory

Z^{c,br}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{C},\lambda,h,s})\in\mathcal{F}^{c}(\mathcal{C})^{Triv}\llbracket\nu\rrbracket

from (
1.3.38
) defines a quantization of the classical backreacted closed string field theor(ies), defined by
L_{N}^{br}(\mathcal{C},\lambda,h)
. We note the intricate
N
-dependence, encoded by the
\nu
-parameter, which is also present in physics
[
79
,
29
]
.

Applications:
we now elaborate on the mentioned relations and dualities in enumerative geometry. We imagine that those could be formulated in the framework of conjecture H;
35
35

35

or perhaps in a refined version of the conjecture, depending on certain parameters; see remark
13.4.2
.
thus on the one hand they provide evidence for the validity of conjecture H and on the other hand could possibly be re-proven if conjecture H would be shown to be true.

•

The finite versions of large
N
open string field theories are large
N
matrix models. The prime example of such comes from the work of Kontsevich
[
67
]
.
He considered the following integral over hermitian
N\times N
matrices and proved that when
N\rightarrow\infty
it can be written, suitably normalized, as a rational polynomial
in variables
tr(\Lambda^{-2i+1}),i\in\mathbb{N}
, where
\Lambda
is just some parameter matrix.

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\mathfrak{h}_{N}}e^{itr(X^{3})}e^{tr(X\Lambda X)}dX\ \in\ \mathbb{Q}[tr(\Lambda^{-1}),tr(\Lambda^{-3}),tr(\Lambda^{-5}),..].

He showed that the coefficients of this polynomial are exactly the intersection numbers on the moduli space of Riemann surfaces. We have seen in theorem
1.1.3
that we can recover the intersection numbers as the partition function of the closed string field theory, as proved by Tu
[
90
]
, when taking the category
\mathcal{C}
to have one object with endomorphisms being the rational numbers. Thus strikingly Kontsevich’s work tells us that
open and closed invariants coincide
, even though the backreaction phenomena seems not to be visible.

At this point we wish to remark that the large
N
matrix model representation of the intersection numbers provided a
major advantage
. Those are amenable by different means as well, for instance formal PDE methods, allowing Kontsevich to prove that the intersection numbers are a logarithm of the
\tau
-function of the KdV-hierarchy, thus verifying Witten’s conjecture
[
96
]
.

•

An analogous instance of a large
N
matrix model computes r-spin intersection numbers
[
9
]
. Those numbers, also known as the FJRW invariants of
\frac{x^{r}}{1+r}
are (after identifying them with the B-model LG invariants, known as Givental-Saito theory, by applying LQ mirror symmetry, proved in
[
63
]
) also conjecturally encoded by the closed Gromov-Witten potential of the Calabi-Yau category of matrix factorizations, see
[
17
]
for a verification in low genus. Again, we do not see any backreaction phenomena here.

Interestingly there is no
N
-dependence on the closed side for the two previous examples contrary to conjecture H; further there was no backreaction phenomena visible. This is different with the next example.

•

An important inspiration for conjecture H is the work of
[
55
]
, see also
[
44
]
for a more recent approach. Gopakumar-Vafa propose that the partition function of large
N
Chern-Simons on
S^{3}
, which is an infinite dimensional matrix model (a gauge theory) exactly encodes the Gromov-Witten invariants of the resolved conifold, described as the backreacted geometry of
S^{3}
in the direction of the zero-section. The zero section is a Lagrangian of
T^{*}S^{3}
, that is an object
\lambda
of
Fuk(T^{*}S^{3})
. On the other hand the observables of large
N
Chern-Simons theory can be described
[
54
,
60
,
61
]
36
36

36

See also
[
26
]
,
[
25
]
with applications to string topology.
by applying the open BD algebra
\mathcal{F}^{o}(\lambda)
to the ‘cyclic’ differential graded algebra of de Rham form on
S^{3}
, which computes
End_{Fuk(T^{*}S^{3})}(\lambda)
, compare
[
1
]
. We should note that in the quantization of large
N
Chern-Simons theory a so called framing anomaly is present
[
5
,
68
,
64
]
. The treatment of
[
60
]
(following
[
32
]
) is modulo constants and does not address this point. The choice of a trivialization of the Chern character in theorem L seems very suggestive in capturing this phenomena. Note, however, that the smoothness assumption is not satisfied for de Rham forms, a comparison for the obtained quantizations may anyways be possible.

•

As mentioned before, Costello-Gaiotto
[
29
]
propose and verify in some aspects a duality between the so called large
N

\beta\gamma
-system and the B-model of the deformed conifold. The latter is described as the backreaction of the closed string field theory of the Calabi-Yau category of coherent sheaves on
\mathbb{C}^{3}
in the direction of the brane
\mathbb{C}\subset\mathbb{C}^{3}
, inducing an object
\lambda\in\mathcal{D}^{b}_{dg}(\mathbb{C}^{3})
of the bounded category of coherent sheaf. The B-model, BCOV invariants of the deformed conifold, are conjecturally computed by
Z^{c,br}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{C},\lambda,h,s})
given the choice of the Beltrami-differential for the primitive of
ch(\lambda)
(see lemma 1 of
[
29
]
) and the splitting determined by the complex conjugate; compare
[
19
]
, page 9. On the other hand the large
N

\beta\gamma
-system is the open string field theory on the brane
\mathbb{C}\subset\mathbb{C}^{3}
; see appendix E of
[
29
]
.

Costello-Gaiotto focus on local aspects of their proposed duality; conjecture H would be about the partition functions of large
N

\beta\gamma
-system and the partition function of the closed SFT of the B-model of the deformed conifold.

Costello-Gaiotto conjecture that their proposed duality describes a subsector of the celebrated
AdS_{5}/CFT_{4}
correspondence in physics
[
79
]
.

Notably in the two last examples listed there is an extremely rich local structure present on both sides (mathematically this can be captured by the notion of factorization algebras
[
30
]
), which is supposed to be matched by holography as well. For instance in
[
29
]
the open side carries the structure of a vertex operator algebra. We contrast this with conjecture H, which is about the global structure. It would be highly interesting to eventually combine these two threads together.

Given the impact of mirror symmetry in the field of mathematics and its origin in physics one can wonder whether holography - phenomenally influential in physics - could also eventually become a rich source of inspiration for mathematics. See
[
34
,
39
,
48
,
47
]
for deep developments in this direction.

2
Overview over the Chapters

In section 3 we put some of the results of this thesis in a broader context by reviewing related works.

•

In section 3.1 we remind the reader of Costello’s work
[
36
]
on topological conformal field theories, the notions of modular,
S^{1}
-framed modular operads and cyclic operads and of the notion of totalization.

•

In section 3.2 we comment on Barannikov’s work on modular operads
[
6
]
.

The main part of this thesis is organized as follows:

•

In section 4 we introduce some algebraic preliminaries. Section 4.1 is dedicated to BD and shifted Poisson algebras, section 4.2 to (cyclic)
L_{\infty}
-algebras and section 4.3 to
A_{\infty}
-categories, Hochschild and cyclic homology. In section 4.4 we introduce Calabi-Yau structures and in section 4.5 splittings of the non-commutative Hodge filtration.

•

In section 5.1 and 5.2 we recall the definition of free closed SFT associated to a smooth cyclic
A_{\infty}
-category and define the notion of interacting closed SFT. In section 5.3 and 5.4 we compare these notions with BCOV theory when applied to the B-model, for the interacting case in terms of conjecture
5.3.19
.

•

In section 6 we introduce and recall shifted Poisson and BD structures induced from cyclic
A_{\infty}
-categories and their quantizations. We study carefully the effect of restricting to essentially finite cyclic
A_{\infty}
-categories.

•

In section 7.1 and 7.2 we recall shifted Poisson and BD structures induced from cyclic
L_{\infty}
-algebras and their quantizations. In section 7.3 we study a functor from
A_{\infty}
-categories to
A_{\infty}
-algebras; in section 7.4 we specialize to finite cyclic
A_{\infty}
-categories.

•

Section 8.1 is about setting up the LQT map for
A_{\infty}
-categories and proving the large
N
statement. We explain how to incorporate the shifted Poisson and BD algebra structures in section 8.2.

•

In section 9.1 we study shifted Poisson and BD algebras built from various ribbon graph complexes; in section 9.2 we do the same but for various (ordinary) graph complexes. Further we identify certain non-trivial Maurer-Cartan elements. In section 9.3 we connect the previous two subsections.

•

In section 10 we explain the generalized Kontsevich’s cocycle construction, both in the commutative and non-commutative setting. It connects the (ribbon) graphs with the algebraic structures induced from cyclic
A_{\infty}
-categories and cyclic
L_{\infty}
-algebras.

•

In section 11 we show how the LQT map, graph complexes and generalized Kontsevich cocycle construction fit together, and how quantum and classical setting are connected.

•

Section 12 is about the open-closed circle action formality morphism, depending on a splitting of the non-commutative Hodge filtration.

•

Section 13 is about ideas how to phrase a ‘Twisted Holography’ duality
[
29
]
at the level of partition functions, given as input a Calabi-Yau category and one of its objects.

3
Some Related Works

In this section we wish to recall some related works and begin by
[
36
]
. Even though we do not directly use its results in this thesis it provides the backbone of much of the theory we study. In particular it is crucial in proving theorem
1.2.7
about the closed representing map, which we motivate and explain with proposition
3.2.15
. Similar this theory should give a different view on theorem B from the introduction about the open representing map, which we indicate around proposition
3.2.11
. To do this we recall the notions of cyclic and modular operads. In the last section we comment on the relation to Barannikov’s work
[
6
]
.

3.1
Topological Conformal Field Theories

We begin by recalling the work of
[
36
]
.
Here, a crucial input is the symmetric monoidal category, depending on a set
\Lambda
of ‘branes’,

\mathscr{O}_{\Lambda}^{d}:=C_{*}(\mathcal{M}^{o}_{\Lambda},\mathcal{L}^{d}),

(3.1.1)

from
[
36
]
, definition 3.0.2, which is given as follows. Its objects are tuples of tuples of
\Lambda
like

(\lambda_{1}^{a},\lambda_{1}^{b}),\cdots,(\lambda_{n}^{a},\lambda_{n}^{b})

for
\lambda_{i}^{x}\in\Lambda,
the monoidal structure
\cup
induced by disjoint union. The Hom spaces of
\mathscr{O}_{\Lambda}^{d}
are built from chains on the moduli space of (not necessarily connected) stable Riemann surfaces with boundary
and
ordered
intervals embedded into the boundary, the free boundaries decorated by objects of a set
\Lambda
and with coefficients in a certain line bundle
\mathcal{L}^{d}
. One imposes the condition that there is at least one free boundary on each boundary component. The composition is induced by gluing together surfaces along open boundaries with matching boundary conditions, as shown in the figure below.

Remark 3.1.2
.

We emphasize that here we are considering ordered embedded intervals, which is crucial for the symmetric monoidal structure. The open BD algebra (
1.3.4
) that we considered before was built from the same moduli space, only that we considered
unordered
embedded intervals. One goal of this section is to explain how the BD algebra (
1.3.4
) is related to the category
\mathscr{O}_{\Lambda}^{d}
.

Figure 1:
An example of composition in
\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda}
, from
[
36
]

Definition 3.1.3

(
[
36
]
, section 5)
.

An open topological conformal field theory (TCFT) is a symmetric monoidal functor

F:(\mathscr{O}_{\Lambda}^{d},\cup)\rightarrow(Ch,\otimes),

which is split.

Theorem 3.1.4

(
[
36
]
, theorem A.1)
.

There is an equivalence of categories between the category of dimension
d
cyclic
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{C}
with set of objects
B
and the category of open TCFT’s

F:(\mathscr{O}_{\Lambda}^{d},\cup)\rightarrow(Ch,\otimes).

The open TCFT
F_{\mathcal{C}}
corresponding under above theorem to a cyclic
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{C}
satisfies

F_{\mathcal{C}}\big((\lambda_{1}^{a},\lambda_{1}^{b}),\cdots,(\lambda_{n}^{a},\lambda_{n}^{b})\big)=Hom_{\mathcal{C}}(\lambda_{1}^{a},\lambda_{1}^{b})\otimes\cdots\otimes Hom_{\mathcal{C}}(\lambda_{n}^{a},\lambda_{n}^{b}).

(3.1.5)

In a similar way Costello
[
36
]
defines another symmetric monoidal category (definition 3.0.2)

\mathscr{C}^{d}:=C_{*}(\mathcal{M}^{c},\mathcal{L}^{d}),

(3.1.6)

its object given by finite sets (think a disjoint union of circles) and
its Hom-spaces built from chains on the moduli space of stable Riemann surfaces with ordered framed boundary and with coefficients in a certain line bundle. One imposes the condition that there is always at least one incoming framed boundary.

Similarly one defines a symmetric monoidal category (definition 3.0.2 of
[
36
]
)

\mathscr{OC}^{d}_{\Lambda}:=C_{*}(\mathcal{M}^{oc}_{\Lambda},\mathcal{L}^{d}),

(3.1.7)

by basically combining the previous ones (see section 1.2 of
[
36
]
). Here one imposes that there is always at least one free boundary or one incoming closed boundary.

Definition 3.1.8

(
[
36
]
, section 5)
.

A closed TCFT is a symmetric monoidal functor

F:(\mathscr{C}^{d},\cup)\rightarrow(Ch,\otimes)

which is split.
An open-closed topological conformal field theory TCFT is a symmetric monoidal functor

F:(\mathscr{OC}_{B}^{d},\cup)\rightarrow(Ch,\otimes)

which is split.

Theorem 3.1.9

(theorem A.1 and A.2,
[
36
]
)
.

Given an open TCFT, corresponding under theorem
3.1.4
to a cyclic
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{C}
, there is an associated universal open-closed TCFT
\Phi
such that given an object of
\mathscr{C}^{d}
(ie. a set
C
) we have

\Phi(C)\simeq CH_{*}(\mathcal{C})^{|C|},

where
CH_{*}(\mathcal{C})
denotes the Hochschild chains of the category
\mathcal{C}.

We also get an induced closed TCFT with the same property.

3.2
Cyclic and Modular Operads

In this section 3.2 we work only with a
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
-grading as we have not checked the degrees and some shifts carefully and take for simplicity
\Lambda=\{*\}
. We nevertheless wished to include this chapter as it provides a more conceptual explanation for eg. theorem B (as we explain at the end of section
3.2.1
) and a heuristic for theorem
1.2.7
(as we explain at the end of section
3.2.2
).

We recall from
[
53
]
the notion of cyclic and of modular operad and from
[
42
]
, definition 1.15 the notion of an odd shifted modular operad.

Example 3.2.1
.

For
d
odd, considering the connected parts of the Hom-spaces of
\mathscr{O}^{d}
with zero outgoing intervals defines an odd shifted modular operad;
the partial composition and contraction maps are defined by gluing with the disc with two outgoing intervals, see section 6 of
[
36
]
and also the treatment of
[
62
]
. By abuse of notation we denote this odd shifted modular operad also by
\mathscr{O}^{d}
.
The genus zero part of
\mathscr{O}^{d}
defines an odd shifted cyclic operads.

An open TCFT induces in particular an algebra
End(V)_{n}:=\big(V^{\vee}[-1]\big)^{\otimes n}
over the odd shifted modular operad; the partial composition and contraction maps (recalling definition 1.15 of
[
42
]
) are defined by pairing with the inverse of the non-degenerate pairing coming from the image of the disk with two outgoing intervals; see lemma 7.3.4 of
[
36
]
for comparison.

Remark 3.2.2
.

In fact the same statements are not true for

\mathscr{OC}^{d}
and
\mathscr{C}^{d}
. If we consider a surface with two incoming and no outgoing framed boundaries nor free boundaries or embedded intervals and we glue together these two incoming framed boundaries we would land outside of
\mathscr{C}^{d}
, as we required positive number of inputs. For
\mathscr{O}^{d}
the situation is different because gluing two open intervals always results in a free boundary. The requirement of positive input for closed TCFTs is crucial to prove theorem
3.1.9
above. The closed TCFT induced from a cyclic
A_{\infty}
-category can in general not be extended to zero inputs. However the results of
[
78
]
indicate that this should be possible if we additionally ask our category to be
smooth
. See also later around diagram
3.2.16
later.

Next, we recall from
[
42
]
, definition 2.1 the notion of odd shifted dg
\Delta
Lie algebras:

Definition 3.2.3
.

An
odd shifted dg
\Delta
-Lie algebra
consists of a chain complex
(\mathfrak{g},d)
, a degree
1
linear operator

\Delta:\mathfrak{g}\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}
, and a degree
1
symmetric product
\{\_,\_\}:\mathfrak{g}^{\odot 2}\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}

satisfying the following relations:

\Delta^{2}=0\ ,\qquad d\Delta+\Delta d=0\ ,\quad d\ \text{and}\ \Delta\ \ \text{are derivations of }\ \ \{\_,\_\}\ ,

and we ask that the shifted Jacobi identity for
\{\_,\_\}
holds.

Further in lemma 2.8 of
[
42
]
a functor is introduced

Tot:\text{odd shifted modular operads}\rightarrow\text{odd shifted dg $\Delta$ Lie algebras}

(3.2.4)

which restricts to

Tot_{cyc}:\text{odd shifted cyclic operads}\rightarrow\text{dg odd shifted Lie algebras.}

(3.2.5)

In fact we could have factored some of our constructions in this thesis through shifted dg
\Delta
Lie algebras by the following functor, as we will see momentarily.

Lemma 3.2.6
.

Given an odd shifted dg
\Delta
Lie algebras

(\mathfrak{g},d,\Delta,\{\_,\_\})
defining

BD(\mathfrak{g}):=Sym(\mathfrak{g})\llbracket\gamma\rrbracket,

where
\gamma
is a formal variable of even degree we equip
BD(\mathfrak{g})
with a BD algebra structure

\Big(BD(\mathfrak{g}),\cdot,d+\gamma\Delta+\gamma\delta_{e},\{\_,\_\}\Big)

where
d
and
\Delta
denote the differentials extended as derivations and
\delta_{e}
the Chevalley-Eilenberg differential of the Lie bracket, further
\{\_,\_\}
denoting the Lie bracket extended
\gamma
-linearly and according to the Leibniz rule. We find a functor

\displaystyle Fr^{\gamma}:\text{odd shifted dg $\Delta$ Lie algebras}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\text{BD algebras}

(3.2.7)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}

\displaystyle\mapsto BD(\mathfrak{g}),

whose
\gamma=0
part gives a functor

Fr:\text{odd shifted dg Lie algebras}\rightarrow\text{shifted Poisson algebras}.

Remark 3.2.8
.

Compare also
[
43
]
, where a functor

\text{odd shifted modular operads with connected sum}\rightarrow\text{BD algebras}

is constructed, which should be a finer construction than to just consider the free algebra on the totalization as in lemma
3.2.7
.

3.2.1
Open Sector

As remarked at the beginning of the last section given an open TCFT of odd dimension we get an algebra over the shifted modular operad
\mathscr{O}^{d}\rightarrow End(V)
. We can apply the totalization functor
3.2.4
, followed by the functor from lemma
3.2.7
, which results in a map of BD algebras

Fr^{\gamma}(Tot(\mathscr{O}^{d}))\rightarrow Fr^{\gamma}(Tot(End(V)))

(3.2.9)

It is easy to see that

Fr^{\gamma}(Tot(\mathscr{O}^{d}))=M^{0},

where
M^{0}
denotes the BD algebra (
1.3.4
) for
\Lambda=\{*\}.

Further we can identify

Tot(End(V))=\Big(Sym(V[-1]^{\vee}),d,\Delta_{o},\{\_,\_\}_{o}\Big),

where
d
is the derivation induced from the underlying differential of the chain complex
(V,d)
and the BV Laplacian
\Delta
and the shifted Poisson bracket
\{\_,\_\}
are the standard ones on Lie algebra cochains of a complex with non-degenerate pairing; see around
7.2.1
. Then it follows that

Fr^{\gamma}(Tot(\mathscr{O}^{d}))=\Big(Sym(Sym(V[-1]^{\vee}))\llbracket\gamma\rrbracket,\cdot,d+\gamma\Delta+\gamma\delta_{e},\{\_,\_\}_{o}\Big),

where
\delta_{e}
denotes the Chevalley-Eilenberg differential associated to
\{\_,\_\}_{o}
. The multiplication gives a map of BD algebras

\Big(Sym(Sym(V[-1]^{\vee}))\llbracket\gamma\rrbracket,\cdot,d+\gamma\Delta_{o}+\gamma\delta_{e},\{\_,\_\}_{o}\Big)\rightarrow\Big(Sym(V[-1]^{\vee})\llbracket\gamma\rrbracket,\cdot,d+\gamma\Delta_{o},\{\_,\_\}_{o}\Big)\cong\mathcal{O}^{pq}(V).

Summarizing, the map (
3.2.9
) composed with multiplication induces
37
37

37

We ignored here the difference between the formal variables
\hbar
and
\gamma
; compare section
8.2
.
a map, denoted

j:\ M^{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}^{pq}(V).

(3.2.10)

It is not difficult to see that we can actually, by the LQT map of rank 1 (recalling the definition of its domain from
1.3.1
)

LQT_{1}:\ \mathcal{F}^{pq}(V)\rightarrow\mathcal{O}^{pq}(V),

factor the map (
3.2.10
) through a map

M^{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}^{pq}(V),

since we get an induced symmetric ordering of cyclic orderings of the tensors in the image of the map
j
from (
3.2.10
), coming from that of symmetric ordering of cyclic orderings of the embedded intervals into the boundaries of a surface.

Precomposing with the quasi-isomorphism of BD algebras mentioned in (
1.3.7
)

M^{0}\simeq\mathcal{RG}\llbracket\gamma\rrbracket

we have shown the following:

Proposition 3.2.11
.

Given an open TCFT
F
, for simplicity with
\Lambda=\{*\}
, so that
F(*,*)=V
there is an induced map of BD algebras

\tilde{\rho}_{F}:\ \mathcal{RG}\llbracket\gamma\rrbracket\rightarrow\mathcal{F}^{pq}(V),

(3.2.12)

induced from the totalization functor
3.2.4
.

Claim

(‘Conjecture’)
.

The map (
3.2.12
), given an open TCFT, coincides with the map

\rho_{A}:\ \mathcal{RG}\llbracket\gamma\rrbracket\rightarrow\mathcal{F}^{pq}(V)

from theorem B applied to the cyclic
A_{\infty}
-algebra
A
corresponding to the open TCFT under theorem
3.1.4
.

Up to signs and possible gradings, this claim should follow from Costello’s work, eg. proposition 6.2.1 of
[
36
]
and generally section 6.2 of
[
36
]
, see also
[
35
]
and may be straightforward to experts.
We did not check the details on signs and gradings and thus formulated the above as a conjecture.

3.2.2
Closed Sector:
S^{1}
-framed modular operads

We recall the notion of an
S^{1}
-framed modular operads from
[
89
]
, also called
S^{1}
-equivariant modular operads, there. Using this notion, we explain in this section the origin of the fundamental closed representing map from the universal closed BD algebra to the algebraic closed BD algebra from theorem
1.2.7
.

Lemma 3.2.13

(sections 2.4 and 5.1 of
[
89
]
)
.

There is a functor

Tot_{S^{1}}:\ \text{$S^{1}$-framed modular operads}\rightarrow\text{dg $\Delta$ shifted Lie algebras},

(3.2.14)

whose underlying chain complex is given by taking the homotopy
S^{1}
-quotient with respect to an
S^{1}
-action; for the algebraic structure we refer to sections 2.4 and 5.1 of
[
89
]
).

Recalling the definitions from section 3.1, let us say extended closed (or extended open-closed) TCFT if we do not impose any positive input condition.
Not imposing the positive input condition we do recover an
S^{1}
-framed modular operad denoted
\mathscr{C}^{d}
by collecting together the Hom-spaces of
\mathscr{C}^{d}
with zero incoming and only outgoing framed boundaries, see example 2.3 of
[
89
]
(similarly for
\mathscr{OC}^{d}
by collecting together the Hom-spaces of
\mathscr{OC}^{d}
with zero incoming closed and zero outgoing open and only outgoing closed boundaries and incoming open boundaries); compare in contrast remark
3.2.2
.
Applying the functors (
3.2.14
) and (
3.2.7
) we have

Fr^{\gamma}(Tot_{S^{1}}(\mathscr{C}^{d}))=M^{c},

the BD algebra from (
1.2.6
), see
[
89
]
. Further given an extended closed TCFT we find an algebra over the
S^{1}
-framed modular operad
\mathscr{C}^{d}
. Let’s assume that a closed TCFT induced from a cyclic
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{C}
by theorem
3.1.9
extends to an extended closed TCFT, which we denote
\Phi
.
It then follows (basically by definition) that

Tot_{S^{1}}(\Phi)=\Big(Sym(CH_{*}(\mathcal{C})[u^{-1}]),d_{Hoch}+uB,\Delta_{c},\{\_,\_\}_{c}\Big),

compare the notions from the BD algebra
1.2.1
. Further it follows that

Fr^{\gamma}(Tot_{S^{1}}(\Phi))=\Big(Sym(Sym(CH_{*}(\mathcal{C})[u^{-1}]))\llbracket\gamma\rrbracket,\cdot,d_{Hoch}+uB+\gamma\Delta_{c}+\gamma\delta_{e},\{\_,\_\}_{c}\Big).

The multiplication induces a map of BD algebras

\displaystyle\Big(Sym(Sym(CH_{*}(\mathcal{C})[u^{-1}]))

\displaystyle\llbracket\gamma\rrbracket,\cdot,d_{Hoch}+uB+\gamma\Delta_{c}+\gamma\delta_{e},\{\_,\_\}_{c}\Big)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\Big(Sym(CH_{*}(\mathcal{C})[u^{-1}])\llbracket\gamma\rrbracket,\cdot,d_{Hoch}+uB+\gamma\Delta_{c},\{\_,\_\}_{c}\Big)\cong\mathcal{F}^{c}(\mathcal{C}).

Thus we have shown:

Proposition 3.2.15
.

Given a closed TCFT induced from a cyclic
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{C}
(by theorem
3.1.9
) which extends to zero inputs there is a map of BD algebras

M^{c}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}^{c}(\mathcal{C}),

induced from the functor
3.2.14
.

Of course, the idea of proposition
3.2.15
is already well-known; see for instance the published version of
[
38
]
, section 3.4. As remarked before, the claim of Lurie
[
78
]
suggest that given a
smooth
cyclic
A_{\infty}
-category one can extend the induced TCFT’s from theorem
3.1.9
to zero inputs. As far as the author is aware, this has not yet been explicitly constructed. However, the idea is used to central effect in theorem
1.2.7
from the introduction, which we here spell out a bit more:

Theorem

(
[
19
]
)
.

Given a smooth cyclic
A_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{C}
there is a map in the homotopy category of dg Lie algebra

M^{c}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}^{c}(\mathcal{C}),

given by a zig-zag of dg Lie algebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{c}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}^{c}(\mathcal{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{M^{c}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathcal{F}^{c}}(\mathcal{C}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

(3.2.16)

The dg-Lie algebras
\widehat{M^{c}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{F}^{c}}(\mathcal{C})
are resolutions of
M^{c}
and
\mathcal{F}^{c}(\mathcal{C})
(the latter only if
\mathcal{C}
is smooth), see section 6.1 of
[
19
]
for details.

Amorim-Tu
[
4
]
are currently writing up an open-closed version (theorem
13.1.5
) of diagram (
3.2.16
).

3.2.3
Barannikov’s work

Theorem 1 of
[
6
]
(see also the very good exposition
[
11
]
, section 8.2.1.) describes, given a modular operad
\mathcal{P}
, a bijection between:

•

algebras on a chain complex
V
over an operad built from the Feynman transform of
\mathcal{P}
,

•

Maurer-Cartan elements in a dg shifted Lie algebra built out of
V
and
\mathcal{P}
.

In section 10 of
[
6
]
the author constructs an explicit modular operad
\mathbb{S}[t]
.
In theorem 2 of loc.cit. he shows that the Feynman transform of
\mathbb{S}[t]
is, after applying a totalization functor similar as in the previous subsection, isomorphic to the stable ribbon graph complex
s\mathcal{RG}
, at least when restricting to the part without leaves.
Furthermore, restricting to the cyclic operad part of
\mathbb{S}[t]
recovers in the similar way
\mathcal{RT}
, see eg.
[
53
]
5.9 and section 9 of
[
6
]
.
The dg shifted Lie algebra he describes, associated to
\mathbb{S}[t]
and a chain complex
V
, is basically
\mathcal{F}^{pq}(V)
considered in this thesis and its cyclic part reduces to
\mathcal{F}^{fr}(V)
, see around equation 6.1 of loc.cit.

Remark
.

One could expect that the outer part of diagram (
1.3.20
) can be obtained as a corollary of Barannikov’s result after applying a totalization functor, analogous to the previous subsection.
It is interesting to note the existence of Maurer-Cartan elements in the induced dg-Lie algebras.

A similar construction may be available for the commutative side:
That is one may ponder the existence of a modular operad, analogous to
\mathbb{S}[t]
, which is a modular extension of
Com
and whose Feynman transform gives
s\mathcal{G}
.
The LQT map may then be derived as induced from a Morita invariance map and a map from
\mathbb{S}[t]
to this modular generalization of
Com
, which would naturally explain our compatibility result, eg. theorem E.

4
Algebraic Preliminaries

In this section we introduce the fundamental notions that we will work with: Shifted Poisson and Beilinson-Drinfeld algebras, (cyclic)
L_{\infty}
-algebras and their cohomology theories, (Calabi-Yau)
A_{\infty}
-categories, Hochschild and cyclic homology and splittings of the non-commutative Hodge filtration.

For the rest of this thesis we fix following conventions. We denote by
k
a field of characteristic 0, unless remarked otherwise. Furthermore we work in the category of chain complexes over
k
, denoted
Ch
, and stick to the homological convention.
The suspension
[\_]
of a chain complex
V
is defined in such a way that if
V
is concentrated in degree zero, then V[1] is concentrated in degree
-1
.

4.1
Shifted Poisson Algebras and Beilinson-Drinfeld Algebras

We follow section 1.4.2 of
[
16
]
for the following definition.

Definition 4.1.1
.

An
r
-shifted Poisson differential graded algebra
is a differential graded, not necessarily unital, k-algebra such that

•

the differential has degree 1

•

it has a Poisson bracket of degree
r
, that is it is a Lie bracket of degree
r
that fulfills the Leibniz rule with respect to the product and is compatible with the differential.

A
map of shifted Poisson dg algebras
is both a map of the underlying dg-algebras and the shifted Lie algebras.

We denote by
(Poiss_{r})
the category whose objects are
r
-shifted Poisson algebras and whose morphisms are maps of
r
-shifted Poisson algebras.

Remark 4.1.2
.

Note that by forgetting the multiplication a shifted Poisson dg algebra forgets to a dg shifted Lie algebra.
When we say Maurer-Cartan element (MCE) of a shifted Poisson dg algebra we refer to a MCE of this dg shifted Lie algebra.
We can
twist
both this dg shifted Lie algebra as well as the overlying shifted Poisson algebra by such a MCE.

We have in mind that
r
denotes an odd integer
38
38

38

Since then we understand the quantized LQT map.
in the following, even though we don’t need to require that at this point. We make following definitions:

Definition 4.1.3
.

An
r
-twisted Beilinson-Drinfeld (
BD
) algebra
is a graded commutative unital algebra over the ring
k\llbracket\mu\rrbracket
of formal power series in a variable
\mu
, which has cohomological degree
1-r
such that

•

it is endowed with a Poisson bracket
\{\_,\_\}
of degree
r
, which is
k\llbracket\mu\rrbracket
-linear

•

it is endowed with an
k\llbracket\mu\rrbracket
-linear map
d
of degree 1 that squares to zero

•

and such that

d(a\cdot b)=d(a)\cdot b+(-1)^{|a|}a\cdot d(b)+\mu\{a,b\}.

(4.1.4)

Remark 4.1.5
.

We will also encounter
r
-twisted
BD
algebras whose multiplication has even degree
m
.
Here we require the formal variable to have degree
1-r+m
.
Note that we could always reduce to the previous definition by performing a shift.
39
39

39

We do not do so since then the quantized LQT map would have non zero degree.

We do not want to introduce additional notation for this case.

Remark 4.1.6
.

In
[
16
]
, section 3.5.1, the notion of a
BD_{d}
algebra is introduced.
It is not clear to the author what is the relationship of this notion and ours, in general.
However, for
r=1
an
r
-twisted BD algebra is the same as a
BD_{0}
algebra.

Remark 4.1.7
.

Since in this thesis we will only deal with
r
-twisted
BD
-algebras we will sometimes refer to them as simply BD algebras as well.

Remark 4.1.8
.

Note that by forgetting the multiplication a BD algebra forgets to a dg shifted Lie algebra.
When we say Maurer-Cartan element of a BD algebra we refer to a MCE of this dg shifted Lie algebra.
Note that we can
twist
not only this dg shifted Lie algebra, but also the overlying BD algebra by such a MCE.

We will only see maps of BD algebras which have the same dimension.
However, we will encounter maps of BD algebras which do not directly respect the algebra structure, but do so up to a certain weighting and live over different base rings:

Definition 4.1.9
.

Let A be an
s
-twisted
BD
algebra over
k\llbracket\gamma\rrbracket
and B be an
s
-twisted
BD
algebra over
k\llbracket\hbar\rrbracket
.
Given
t\in\mathbb{N}
we say that a
t
-weighted map of BD algebras
is given by a map of algebras

g:k\llbracket\gamma\rrbracket\rightarrow k\llbracket\hbar\rrbracket

such that

g(\gamma)=\hbar^{t}

(4.1.10)

and a map of
k\llbracket\gamma\rrbracket
dg shifted Lie algebras

f:A\rightarrow g^{*}B

such that

f(a_{1}\cdot\ldots\cdot a_{k})=\hbar^{(t-1)(k-1)}f(a_{1})\cdot\ldots\cdot f(a_{k}).

(4.1.11)

Remark 4.1.12
.

For
t=1
we recover the standard definition of a map of BD algebras.
We indicate this by simply dropping the adjective weighted.

Remark 4.1.13
.

Composition of a
t
-weighted map of BD algebras and an
s
-weighted map of BD algebras is an
s\cdot t
-weighted map of BD algebras.

Definition 4.1.14
.

Denote by
(\text{BD}^{r-tw})
the category whose objects are
r
-twisted BD algebras and whose morphisms are maps of BD algebras.

Remark 4.1.15
.

Note that (
4.1.10
) is a necessary condition allowing us to extend g to a map

\tilde{g}:k(\!(\gamma)\!)\rightarrow k(\!(\hbar)\!)

where
\tilde{g}(r\cdot\gamma^{-k})=g(r)g(\gamma)^{-k}.

We further get an induced map

\tilde{f}:A(\!(\gamma)\!)\rightarrow\tilde{g}^{*}B(\!(\hbar)\!).

Condition (
4.1.11
) implies that for any
x\in A
we have

\tilde{f}e^{x/\gamma}=\hbar^{1-t}e^{f(x)/\hbar}.

(4.1.16)

This guarantees commutativity of
